# Parameter tuning log

Generated by `python -m backtest.tune`. See that module's docstring for the full methodology (period split, grid rationale, why parallel, why not a finer sweep).

## ⚠️ Read this before the results below: position sizing & mechanics audit (historical, pre-rebuild)

**This section describes the pre-2026-09-rebuild system** - the old 4-vote
aggregator, `min_confluence` on a 0-4 scale, `min_break_distance` as a live swept
parameter, and a 256-combination grid that no longer exists. Kept here as
provenance for *why* `skip_if_capital_capped=True` became a fixed (non-swept)
default in every grid this project has run since (including the current
1215-combination one below) - not as a current-system result. See "Clean
short-side diagnostic" further down for the post-rebuild, post-sizing-fix re-run
of the short-side question this section originally raised.

Before concluding anything about this strategy's edge from a grid's results,
three questions were audited directly against `backtest/risk.py`/`backtest/runner.py`
as actually used at the time - because a substantial part of a -98% median result
turned out to be explained by the harness's own sizing/fee mechanics, not by the
strategy's directional calls being wrong.

**1. Position sizing: 1% of *current* (compounding) equity per trade, sized against
the spot purchasing-power cap when the intended risk-based size isn't affordable.**
`RiskManager.calculate_size()` computes `risk_amount = current_balance * risk_pct`
(`risk_pct` defaults to `0.01`) - `current_balance` is the same mutable
`RiskManager` instance `FillEngine` updates after every trade
(`self.risk.current_balance += pnl` in `_check_exit`), so sizing compounds on
whatever the account has left, not a fixed initial balance.
`RiskManager.apply_result()` - a separate WIN/LOSS-based PnL path - exists in the
file but is **never called** anywhere in the actual backtest path; confirmed by
`grep`.

The consequential part: when the intended risk-based quantity
(`risk_amount / stop_distance`) would cost more than the account can afford (no
margin on Spot), `calculate_size()` silently clips to
`max_affordable_qty = (current_balance * 0.98) / entry_price` - **not** a rejection.
Empirically, on the single best-ROI combination in the grid (`min_confluence=4`,
`min_ob_body_ratio=2.0`, `min_fvg_gap_ratio=2.0`, `min_break_distance=100.0`), **this
cap bound 63.5% of all 74 trades** (47 of them) - meaning the position was pushed to
~98% of account notional on nearly two-thirds of trades, regardless of how tight the
"intended" 1%-risk stop distance was supposed to make it.

**2. Realized stop-loss losses match the intended price distance - the simulator
does not model gap-through risk, if anything it's optimistic there. But total
dollar loss per trade can still exceed the "1% risk" target by a wide margin,
because fees scale with the (often-capped) notional, not with the intended risk.**
`_check_exit()` always fills a stopped-out position at exactly `pos.stop_loss`
(plus a fixed ~5bps slippage haircut), regardless of how far a candle's actual low/
high undershot/overshot that level - so a real gap-down through the stop would, in
reality, likely fill worse than this simulator ever models. That is a real
limitation (see the kickoff's "local-simulator paper trading still isn't real
execution" caveat) but it is *conservative in the optimistic direction*: it is not
the source of the -98% figure.

The actual source: fees (Kraken's ~0.40% taker fee, charged on entry AND exit
notional) don't know or care what the "intended" 1% risk was - they're charged on
`entry_price * size`. When the purchasing-power cap forces `size` up to ~98% of the
account (see above) while the stop distance stays tight, the round-trip fee bill can
be **larger than the entire intended risk amount for that trade**. On the same
best-ROI combination: total fees paid across all 74 trades were **$7,541.54**
against a total account decline of **$9,697.73** - fees alone account for **~78% of
the entire loss**. The worst single trade realized **-15.33R** (a "1% risk" trade
that lost over 15x its intended risk, purely from fee drag on an oversized capped
position, not from the price move itself - price-level loss was capped correctly at
the stop). This is a genuine sizing-model mismatch, not a bug in the sense of
incorrect arithmetic: the model assumes a tight percentage-risk stop implies a
correspondingly small position, which is true with margin/leverage but not on
unleveraged Spot, where a tight stop can instead just mean an *oversized* position
gets capped in, at full fee exposure. No fix had been applied at the time of this
audit - reported as an audit finding for a deliberate decision, not silently
patched.

**3. Long-only by explicit design in `backtest/runner.py`, not "shorts supported but
didn't fire."** `FillEngine._evaluate_signal()` only ever sets a pending entry on
`signal.direction == "BULLISH"`; a `"BEARISH"` direction was, before this change,
unconditionally discarded (the code comment literally read "no short-selling on
spot - skip"). `backtest/risk.py`'s stop-loss/take-profit math already supported
the short side symmetrically before this change (the bearish branch of
`_structural_stop_loss` and the non-`"BUY"` branch of `take_profit_price` already
existed, just unused) - only `FillEngine`'s entry gating and `_check_exit`'s
direction-aware trigger/PnL logic needed to change. `allow_short` (default `False`,
diagnostic/backtest-only, never wired into `live/trader.py` or paper trading since
Kraken Spot cannot execute a short position at all) was added to measure this.

### Historical: quick single-combination short-side check (pre-fix, pre-rebuild)

> This was a quick check on one combination, run entirely under the *unfixed*
> (forced-sizing), *pre-rebuild* baseline, before committing to a full grid
> re-run. **Superseded by "Clean short-side diagnostic" below**, which re-runs
> the same question on the current system with the sizing fix in place.

Enabling shorts made the single best-known long-only combination *worse*, not
better: **-48.49% (long-only) -> -76.26% (long+short)**. 86 additional short
trades fired at a similar ~17% win rate to the long side, which - given finding 2
above - mostly meant 86 more trades subject to the same fee-drag-on-capped-notional
mechanism, not 86 trades that captured a real move. The full 256-combination
long+short re-run was deliberately not run at the time, given this single-check
signal and finding 2's separate, stronger quantitative evidence.

### Historical: does `skip_if_capital_capped` fix it?

Finding 2 identified the mechanism (fee drag on capital-capped, forced-oversized
positions) but stopped short of fixing it. `RiskManager` gained a
`skip_if_capital_capped` constructor option: when the risk-sized position would
need more capital than the account has, **skip the trade entirely** instead of
silently downsizing to whatever's affordable.

**Single-combination re-run** (best-known combo at the time - `min_confluence=4,
min_ob_body_ratio=2.0, min_fvg_gap_ratio=2.0, min_break_distance=100.0` - same
2025-04-01 -> 2025-12-31 period, same $20,000 initial balance):

| metric | baseline (`skip_if_capital_capped=False`) | fix (`skip_if_capital_capped=True`) |
|---|---|---|
| trades taken | 74 | 29 |
| win rate | 17.6% | 34.5% |
| profit factor | 0.12 | 0.28 |
| ROI | **-48.49%** | **-20.11%** |
| max drawdown | 48.5% | 21.7% |
| capital-cap trigger rate | 63.5% (47 of 74 sizing attempts) | 86.9% (192 of 221, all skipped) |
| approx. total taker fees | $7,550 | $3,074 |

**This substantially closed the gap without reaching profitability** (-48.49% ->
-20.11%) - confirming the sizing/fee mechanism was a real, large driver, not a red
herring, but not the sole one. This is why `skip_if_capital_capped=True` became a
fixed (non-swept) default in every grid run since, including the current
1215-combination one below, which was run entirely under the *post-rebuild*
detectors *and* this fix already in place.

## Clean short-side diagnostic (post-rebuild, post-sizing-fix)

**Supersedes both historical short-side notes above** - those ran under the *old*
detectors and the *unfixed* (forced-sizing) baseline, so "shorts make it worse" was
confounded by two things that have since changed. This is the first apples-to-apples
short-side check on the current system: current 6-detector/5-vote aggregator,
`skip_if_capital_capped=True` (the now-fixed default), the best non-low-sample
detector combination from the 1215-point grid below held fixed
(`min_confluence=5`, `min_ob_body_ratio=0.0`, `min_fvg_gap_ratio=0.0`,
`min_trendline_break_distance=100.0`, `min_channel_break_distance=200.0`,
`max_trap_retest_distance=inf`), same **2025-04-01 -> 2025-12-31** tuning period,
$20,000 initial balance.

**Kept small and separate, not a full-grid re-run**: one fixed combination run
twice (`allow_short=False` vs `allow_short=True`) rather than the full-grid
`--diagnostic-allow-short` path (which would re-run all 1215 combinations twice,
~15+ hours) - this answers "does the short side change the picture for the
best-known combination," the same question the full-grid diagnostic would answer
at over a thousand times the compute cost.

| metric | long-only | long+short |
|---|---|---|
| trades | 26 | 51 (23 long / 28 short) |
| win rate | 61.5% | 51.0% |
| profit factor | 0.80 | 0.51 |
| ROI | **-3.54%** | **-18.92%** |
| max drawdown | 7.58% | 26.54% |
| avg R-multiple | -0.13 | -0.40 |

**Overall: shorts make it meaningfully worse, not better** - the old (confounded)
diagnostic's directional conclusion still holds even with both confounds removed.
Enabling shorts more than triples the drawdown (7.58% -> 26.54%) and turns a small
loss into a much larger one.

**But the picture is not uniform across the period.** Broken out for the specific
**2025-10-06 ($126,073.30 peak) -> 2025-12-31 ($87,500.10, period end)** decline
leg - the one stretch of this window a long-only strategy structurally cannot
profit from:

| metric (this leg only) | long-only | long+short |
|---|---|---|
| trades | 9 | 14 (7 long / 7 short) |
| win rate | 66.7% | 85.7% |
| profit factor | 0.97 | 2.87 |
| net PnL | -$31 (flat) | **+$1,063** |

**During the one real trending decline in the window, shorts clearly help** -
long-only is essentially flat there (as expected), while long+short is strongly
positive. The short side does exactly what it was added to test for.
**The overall result is still worse with shorts because the other 37 short/long
trades outside this leg (51 total - 14 in-leg) lose enough to erase the leg's
gain and then some** - net PnL outside the leg is roughly -$4,848
(-$3,784.59 total - (+$1,063.32 in-leg) = -$4,847.91). The current
BEARISH-signal-to-short logic doesn't distinguish "a real trend is starting" from
noise/chop, so it fires (and loses on) shorts outside the one window where
shorting actually pays off.

**This refines rather than overturns "don't enable shorts on this venue"**: the
mechanism is now understood (shorts work during a real trend, lose during chop,
and this 9-month sample is mostly chop), which is a more actionable finding than
"shorts just don't work here." A trend filter gating when the short side fires is
a plausible future follow-up - not attempted here, this is a diagnostic, not a new
detector. Kraken Spot still cannot execute a short position at all regardless
(see `FillEngine.allow_short`'s docstring) - this remains backtest-only.

## Grid search results

Tuning period: **2025-04-01 -> 2025-12-31** (2025-04-01 - 2025-12-31 by default). 1215 combinations, sorted by ROI (highest first) - full distribution, not a top-N cut. Swept: `min_confluence` (1-5, the aggregator's full 5-vote range), `min_ob_body_ratio`, `min_fvg_gap_ratio`, `min_trendline_break_distance`, `min_channel_break_distance`, `max_trap_retest_distance` - 6 parameters, this is the 2026-09 rebuild's grid (order block/FVG/trendline/channel/fakeout-trap detectors, 5-vote aggregator), not the pre-rebuild one.

- **`min_break_distance` is deliberately excluded from this grid - not deprioritized, removed.** It gates `detect_bos_choch`'s close-crossing threshold, and a direct code trace confirms that detector's output (`structure_bias`/`structure_events`) has no causal path to any backtest result: `signals/smc_aggregator.py`'s 5-vote model (order block/FVG/trendline/channel/fakeout_trap) never reads it, and `backtest/runner.py`'s stop-loss placement reads only `order_blocks`. Sweeping it could not have changed a single result in this table - every combination below implicitly ran with `min_break_distance` at its unswept default (`0.0`).
- **`trendline_points` is fixed at its default (`3`), not swept.** It's structural (changes what points the trendline/channel fits are computed from) rather than a filter threshold - same category as `swing_left_bars`/`swing_right_bars`, also fixed. See `backtest/tune.py`'s module docstring.
- Combinations with fewer than 20 trades are flagged `†` and should be read as low-sample, not as a genuine result - a handful of trades over 9 months of a sparse-signal strategy is not enough to distinguish structure from chance.
- 81/1215 combinations are low-sample (`†`); 0 produced zero trades.
- Trade count across the grid: min 4, median 319, max 525.

See the kickoff notes' caveats on SMC-on-spot signal sparsity, bars-vs-stints sample-size inflation, and "results that look good by chance vs. results with a structural explanation" before treating any row here as a decision. The held-out period (2026-01-01..2026-03-31) is reserved for a single deliberate `--evaluate-holdout` check on whichever combination gets chosen from this table - not for re-running the search.

## Risk-parameter grid: rr_ratio x min_sl_distance (targeted follow-up)

Tuning period: **2025-04-01 -> 2025-12-31**. 16 combinations (4 rr_ratio x 4 min_sl_distance values), sorted by ROI (highest first) - full plateau, not a top-N cut. Kept deliberately separate from the 6-parameter detector grid above rather than swept jointly with it - this checks a different, orthogonal lever (risk management, not signal quality).

Every combination below holds detector/confluence parameters fixed at the best non-low-sample result from the detector grid: `min_confluence=5`, `min_ob_body_ratio=0.0`, `min_fvg_gap_ratio=0.0`, `min_trendline_break_distance=100.0`, `min_channel_break_distance=200.0`, `max_trap_retest_distance=inf` (`skip_if_capital_capped=True`, same fixed sizing behavior as the detector grid).

- Combinations with fewer than 20 trades are flagged `†` (low-sample).
- 0/16 combinations are low-sample.

**Reading this table**: holding min_sl_distance fixed and varying rr_ratio moves ROI by 14.09 points (worst rr_ratio=1.0 -> best rr_ratio=3.0); holding rr_ratio fixed and varying min_sl_distance moves ROI by at most 0.00 points within any single rr_ratio. min_sl_distance had zero measured effect here - every value tested (0 through the largest in MIN_SL_DISTANCE_GRID) produced identical trades/ROI for a given rr_ratio, meaning every realized stop distance in this combination already exceeds the largest floor tested. At BTC's current price scale, `_structural_stop_loss` (backtest/runner.py) places stops via order-block levels or a 1% price buffer - roughly $750-$1,260 across this period's $75k-$126k range - so a floor in the tens-to-low-hundreds of dollars can't bind. A future round anchored on that actual scale (percent-of-price or a much larger dollar floor) would be a fairer test of this parameter than this grid's values; rr_ratio is unambiguously the lever that matters at the values tested here.

## Holdout evaluations

One-time deliberate checks via `python -m backtest.tune --evaluate-holdout ...`, never run automatically. Held-out period: 2026-01-01 -> 2026-03-31.

### 2026-09-04 02:25 UTC

Params: `min_confluence=5`, `min_ob_body_ratio=0.0`, `min_fvg_gap_ratio=0.0`, `min_trendline_break_distance=100.0`, `min_channel_break_distance=200.0`, `max_trap_retest_distance=inf`, `rr_ratio=3.0`, `min_sl_distance=50.0`. Evaluated on 2026-01-01 -> 2026-03-31.

| Trades | Win% | PF | PnL | ROI% | MaxDD% | AvgR | MedR | StdR |
|--- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | ---|
| 21 | 19.0 | 0.33 | -3443 | -17.21 | 19.14 | -0.88 | -1.63 | 1.60 |

**Did not generalize.** In-sample (tuning period, this same combination): 24 trades, 50.0% win rate, PF 1.32, ROI +6.86%. Held-out: 21 trades, **19.0% win rate**, PF 0.33, ROI **-17.21%**. Win rate alone collapsed by 31 points - this isn't a marginal miss, the edge the risk grid found in-sample is not present in the held-out quarter. Per this project's own validation discipline, the held-out period is not to be re-tuned against in response to this result: this is reported as "found something real in-sample that didn't generalize," a legitimate and informative outcome in its own right, not a bug to fix or a reason to widen the search. Nothing in this repository (live trading, paper trading, or the shipped `rr_ratio` default) should be changed on the basis of the in-sample risk-grid result alone - it has now been checked, and it did not hold up.
